Section Seven · A comprehensive glossary of programming, security, and networks

Glossary

A searchable, filterable bilingual glossary: programming terms, cybersecurity, networks, databases, web, and development tools. Each term has its English name and a clear, direct definition — search in English or filter by field.

In simple wordsThe glossary is a quick reference: look up any term in Arabic or English, read a two-line definition, and filter by field.
202 terms Programming · Security · Networking Instant search 6 fields

⌨ Programming

Variables, functions, objects, data structures, algorithms, and design patterns — the foundation on which any application is built.

🛡 Cybersecurity

From vulnerabilities, exploits and phishing to protection, multi-layered defense, and identity and permissions management.

🌐 Networks

Addresses, ports, protocols, routing, DNS, TCP/IP, HTTP — how data actually travels.

Loading...

VariableA name that refers to a value in memory that can be changed. In static languages the type must be declared; in dynamic languages it is inferred automatically.Programming
FunctionA reusable block of code that takes input (Parameters) and returns an output (Return). The goal: reduce redundancy and facilitate testing.Programming
LoopA construct that repeats the execution of code until a condition is met. The most popular ones are for and while. Beware of infinite loops when the stopping condition does not change.Programming
ConditionA logical expression that specifies the path of execution (if/else). Its result is True or False, and can be combined with AND, OR, and NOT operators.Programming
ArrayA collection of elements of the same type in successive positions, accessed by an index that usually starts at zero. Size is fixed in some languages and dynamic in others.Programming
ObjectA unit that groups data (properties) and behavior (methods) together. The object is an instance of a class, and can represent a user, a request, or any concept.Programming
ClassA template or blueprint that defines the properties and methods of objects. It is used to create multiple copies with the same structure and behavior.Programming
InheritanceBuilding a new class on top of an existing one, inheriting its properties and methods, and adding or overriding behavior. Used to reduce duplication and organize code.Programming
PolymorphismThe ability of objects from different classes to respond to the same message in different ways. Achieved via Overriding or Overloading.Programming
EncapsulationHides an object's internal details and exposes them through a specific interface. Protects the state from accidental modification.Programming
AbstractionFocus on what something does rather than how it does it. The interface exposes what it does and hides internal complexity.Programming
InterfaceA contract that specifies the set of methods a class must implement without prescribing how. It allows swapping implementations without changing the caller.Programming
CompilerA program that converts the entire source code into machine code or an intermediate language before running. Detects type errors early, as in C++ and Rust.Programming
InterpreterA program that executes code line by line without prior compilation. Makes it easier to experiment but often slower, like Python and JavaScript.Programming
AlgorithmA series of specific, definitive steps to solve a problem. It is measured by correctness, clarity, and time and space efficiency.Programming
Big ODescribe how algorithm time grows with the size of the input. O(1) is constant, O(log n) is logarithmic, O(n) is linear, O(n²) is quadratic.Programming
RecursionA function that calls itself to solve a smaller version of the problem, with an underlying stopping condition. Useful for trees and problems that can be divided.Programming
ExceptionAn error that occurs during execution and can be caught and handled without terminating the program. Each exception type has a different meaning.Programming
DebuggingThe step-by-step process of identifying the cause of incorrect behavior and fixing it using breakpoints, logs, and variable checking.Programming
Unit TestAn automated test that checks a function or small module in isolation from the rest. It prevents old errors from returning when modifying.Programming
RefactoringImproving the structure of code without changing its external behavior: clearer names, smaller functions, and removal of redundancy.Programming
LibraryA set of ready-made code that you call when needed without controlling the structure of your program. Like requests in Python.Programming
FrameworkA structure that defines an application's architecture and calls your code at specific points (inversion of control), such as Django and React.Programming
RuntimeThe environment in which the code is executed, including the engine, core libraries, and memory management. Such as Node.js and the JVM.Programming
SDKA set of tools, libraries, and documentation that helps you build an application for a specific platform, such as the Android SDK.Programming
HTMLA markup language that defines the structure of a web page and the meaning of its elements: headings, paragraphs, links, forms. It is not a programming language.Web
CSSA stylesheet language that controls appearance: colors, fonts, spacing, and layout. It works with selector rules and declarations.Web
DOMA tree representation of an HTML page in memory. JavaScript edits it and the view changes immediately without reloading the page.Web
SPAA web application that loads a single HTML page and changes content via JavaScript without a full reload. Such as React and Vue.Web
SSRBuilds the full HTML on the server and sends it ready to the browser. Improves first render and SEO compared to CSR.Web
CSRSends a nearly blank page and then builds the content in the browser with JavaScript. Fast interaction after the first load.Web
Responsive DesignA layout that adapts to screen sizes using relative units, flexible images, and media queries.Web
CookieA small file that the browser stores and sends with every request to the same site. Used for sessions, preferences, and tracking.Web
SessionThe period of a user's interaction with a site, usually identified by a session identifier stored in a cookie and linked to data on the server.Web
CORSA browser-enforced policy that prevents a page from reading another domain's responses unless the server sends the appropriate CORS headers.Web
CDNA geographically distributed server network that serves static files from the point closest to the user, reducing download time and load.Web
WebSocketA two-way, persistent communication protocol between browser and server, suitable for chat, streaming and live applications.Web
RESTA method of building APIs based on resources and HTTP methods (GET/POST/PUT/DELETE), and is stateless.Web
GraphQLA query language for APIs that lets a client request only the fields it needs instead of a fixed response.Web
SEOPractices that make the page understandable to search engines: semantic structure, speed, links, and metadata.Web
AccessibilityMaking a site usable for everyone, including people with disabilities: contrast, keyboard navigation, and alternative text.Web
SQLRelational database query language: creating, inserting, modifying, deleting, and retrieving tables with related data.Databases
NoSQLNon-relational databases that store documents, key/value pairs, or graphs; they are flexible and horizontally scalable.Databases
Primary KeyA column or group of columns that uniquely identifies each row and cannot be repeated or left empty.Databases
Foreign KeyA column that points to the primary key in another table, ensuring the integrity of the relationship between the two tables.Databases
IndexA structure that speeds up searches on a particular column in exchange for extra storage and somewhat slower writes.Databases
JOINThe process of merging rows from two tables according to a common relationship. Types: INNER, LEFT, RIGHT, FULL.Databases
TransactionA set of operations that are executed as a single unit: either all of them succeed or all of them are aborted, to ensure data integrity.Databases
ORMA layer that turns tables into objects in code, interacting with the database without writing SQL directly.Databases
MigrationA file that describes a change to the database structure (adding a table or column) and can be implemented and undone in an orderly manner.Databases
GitA version control system that tracks code changes locally and allows branches to be created, merged, and rolled back to any version.Tools
RepositoryA project folder tracked by Git, containing all change history and branches. It may be local or on GitHub.Tools
BranchAn independent line of development that splits from the project history, allowing work on a feature without affecting the main branch.Tools
MergeCombines changes from one branch into another. A conflict may occur if the same line is modified in both branches.Tools
CI/CDContinuous Integration and Delivery: build, test, and deploy code automatically on every change to reduce manual errors.Tools
DockerA tool that packages an application and its dependencies into a container that works the same on any machine.Tools
KubernetesA platform for orchestrating containers: starts them, scales them, restarts them, and distributes load between them automatically.Tools
TerminalA text interface for entering commands into the operating system. Mastering it doubles your speed in development and management.Tools
SSHAn encrypted command line communication protocol with a remote machine over port 22, used to manage servers.Tools
IDEAn integrated development environment that combines editor, debugger, file manager, and terminal into one program, like VS Code.Tools
Environment VariableA value set by the operating system and read by the application, used to keep settings and secrets outside the code.Tools
Package ManagerA tool that downloads, updates, and resolves libraries: pip, npm, cargo, apt.Tools
DomainThe name people type to reach a website, such as example.com. It resolves to an IP via DNS.Networks
SubdomainA name under the primary domain, such as mail.example.com or blog.example.com.Networks
URLThe full address of a page or resource on the Internet, including the protocol, domain, and path (https://site.com/page).Networks
IPThe digital address of a device on the network. Without it, packets do not know where to go.Networks
PortA number that identifies the service inside the device (Web 80/443, SSH 22...). The same IP can run many services on different ports.Networks
ProtocolAgreed rules that let two devices understand each other (HTTP for the web, SSH for access, FTP for files).Networks
DNSA service that translates domain names into IP addresses — the Internet's phone book.Networks
DHCPA service that automatically distributes IP addresses to devices when they connect to the network.Networks
NATA router technology that lets many devices in a home share the same public IP on the Internet.Networks
GatewayDefault gateway: the device (usually the router) through which all traffic leaving the local network passes.Networks
LAN / WANLAN: small local area network. WAN is a wide network like the Internet or connecting cities and branches.Networks
PacketA small piece of data carried on the network. Files and pages are split into packets and reassembled at the other end.Networks
LatencyThe time a packet takes from your device until the response returns. The lower, the better.Networks
BandwidthThe amount of data that can be transferred per second (mega/giga). Not the same as response time.Networks
FirewallFilters allowed and blocked connections according to rules.Networks
ProxyAn intermediary between you and the target. Requests pass through it first (like Burp when testing a website).Networks
VPNAn encrypted tunnel between your device and a server that hides the connection's content from the network you are on.Networks
EncryptionConverting data into a form that cannot be read without a key. HTTPS and VPNs depend on it.Networks
HashA fixed fingerprint of data or a password. It is not easily reversed and is used for secure storage and comparison.Security
SaltA random value added to a password before hashing, so the same password does not produce the same hash for every user.Security
AuthenticationVerifying your identity (user, password, key, fingerprint...).Security
AuthorizationAfter the system knows who you are: what you are allowed to do (permissions).Security
VulnerabilityA weakness in a system or program that an attacker can exploit to do something unintended.Security
ExploitCode or a technique that actually uses a vulnerability to achieve a specific goal.Security
PayloadThe part executed after exploitation (e.g., opening a shell or downloading a file).Security
ShellA command interface on the system. A “reverse shell” means the target connects back to you and you control it.Security
Root / AdminThe highest privilege level on the system. Access to it means almost complete control.Security
Privilege EscalationRaising your privileges from a regular user to a higher level after gaining access to the system.Security
ReconnaissanceCollecting information about a target before attacking or testing (domains, ports, technologies).Security
OSINTCollecting information from public sources (Google, social media, records) without hacking.Security
Brute ForceTrying many possibilities (passwords or keys) until one succeeds.Security
WordlistA file of prepared words or passwords used for guessing or discovery.Security
MITMMan in the middle: the attacker sits between two parties and observes or alters the traffic.Security
ARP SpoofingTricking local devices with a fake MAC address so that traffic is sent to the attacker instead of the router.Networks
Handshake (WPA)The message exchange when a device connects to Wi-Fi. Capturing it is an essential step before attempting to crack the key.Networks
Packet SniffingCapturing and reading network packets (e.g., with Wireshark) for analysis or monitoring.Networks
SQL InjectionInjecting database commands through malicious input in a form or parameter.Security
XSSInjecting a script into a web page that executes in the visitor's browser.Security
CSRFTricking a logged-in user's browser into sending a request to another site without the user's intent.Security
Session HijackingStealing a logged-in user's session ID and using it to impersonate them. Prevention: HTTPS, HttpOnly cookies, post-login ID renewal.Security
HttpOnly / SameSiteCookie properties prevent JavaScript from reading it (HttpOnly), and limit it from being sent with other site requests (SameSite) — an important defense against XSS and CSRF.Security
APIA programming interface that lets programs talk to each other, request data, or execute commands in a structured way.Security
SSL / TLSThe encryption protocols behind HTTPS. They secure the connection between the browser and the server.Networks
CertificateA file that proves a site's identity to the user and is an essential part of HTTPS.Networks
CVEA standard identifier for a published vulnerability, in the form CVE-2024-1234, used to track fixes and compare versions.Security
Zero-dayA vulnerability unknown to the public and the vendor, so no fix is available yet.Security
PatchA developer update that fixes a vulnerability or improves security.Security
SandboxAn isolated environment for running suspicious programs without affecting the host system.Security
ForensicsExamining digital evidence (disks, memory, logs) after an incident to understand what happened.Security
MalwareMalicious software: a virus, Trojan, ransomware... any program designed to harm or spy.Security
PhishingTricking people (with a fake email or page) to steal data or trigger a malicious action.Security
Social EngineeringExploiting trust and human behavior rather than technical vulnerabilities.Security
PentestAn authorized security assessment that simulates a real attack so problems can be fixed before attackers exploit them.Security
ScopeThe agreed testing limits (systems, times, attack types). Going beyond them is illegal.Security
CIA TriadThe three primary security goals: confidentiality, integrity, and availability.Security
Hashing vs EncryptionHashing is one-way, for comparison. Encryption is two-way: encrypt and decrypt with the key.Security
Localhost / 127.0.0.1The address means “my device.” Services listening there are available only from the same device by default.Networks
LoopbackThe same idea as localhost: the network returns to the same device without exiting onto the real network.Networks
SSIDThe name of the Wi-Fi network shown in the network list.Networks
WPA2 / WPA3Wi-Fi security protocols. WPA3 is newer and stronger than WPA2.Networks
Monitor ModeA Wi-Fi card mode that captures all nearby signals, not just the network it is connected to.Networks
Active Directory (AD)Microsoft's system for managing users, permissions, and devices in corporate networks.Security
EndpointAn end device on the network: laptop, phone, server...Networks
LogAn event file that records what happened on the system or application (logins, errors, requests).Security
StackA data structure that follows the LIFO (last in, first out) principle. Used for function calls (the call stack) and undo.Programming
QueueA FIFO (first in, first out) data structure. Used to schedule tasks and process requests.Programming
PointerA variable that holds a memory address rather than the value itself. Common in C and C++, it requires care to avoid invalid access.Programming
Garbage CollectionAn automatic mechanism that frees memory that is no longer used, found in Java, Python, Go, and JavaScript.Programming
ConcurrencyRunning multiple tasks in overlapping intervals to optimize processor utilization. It differs from parallelism, where tasks run at the same instant.Programming
Async / AwaitA way to write code that waits for slow operations (network/files) without freezing the program, while reading like sequential code.Programming
Type SystemRules that define which types of values and operations are allowed. Static typing is checked before running; dynamic typing is checked at runtime.Programming
Design PatternA general, reusable solution to a common design problem, such as Singleton, Factory, and Observer. Not ready-made code but a way of thinking.Programming
Threat ModelA structured analysis of what you are protecting, from whom, and how — before choosing any security measure.Security
Attack SurfaceThe total set of points through which an attacker can reach the system: ports, APIs, accounts, uploaded files.Security
Least PrivilegeThe principle of granting a user or program only the permissions it needs, reducing the impact of any compromise.Security
Defense in DepthApplying independent layers of protection (network, system, application, data) so that breaching one layer is not enough to reach everything.Security
SIEMA system that collects and analyzes device and application logs to detect attack patterns and alert the security team.Security
IDS / IPSIDS monitors traffic and alerts on suspicious activity; IPS blocks it automatically. Both rely on signatures or behavior.Security
HoneypotA decoy system designed to lure attackers and record their methods instead of exposing real systems.Security
RansomwareMalware that encrypts the victim's files and demands a ransom. Prevention: Separate backups and constant updates.Security
Reverse ShellA connection initiated from the compromised device back to the attacker, giving the attacker a command line on it. The opposite of a bind shell.Security
MFARequires at least two different authentication factors: something you know (password), something you have (code/device), or something you are (fingerprint).Security
Subnet MaskA number that identifies which part of an IP address is the network and which part is the device, such as 255.255.255.0.Networks
CIDRA shorthand for writing the mask with a bit suffix, such as /24 instead of 255.255.255.0.Networks
MAC AddressA unique physical address of the network card (48 bits), used within the local network for switching.Networks
OSI ModelA 7-layer model that describes data transmission: physical, data link, network, transport, session, presentation, and application.Networks
TCP / UDPTwo transport protocols: TCP guarantees delivery and ordering; UDP is faster with no guarantees — suitable for streaming and gaming.Networks
HTTP / HTTPSThe web request protocol. HTTPS is HTTP inside an encrypted TLS tunnel that protects data from eavesdropping and tampering.Networks
Router / SwitchA switch connects devices within a local network; a router routes packets between different networks.Networks
Load BalancerIt distributes requests across several servers to avoid overload and maintain availability when a server fails.Networks
RoutingThe process of choosing the path a packet takes between networks based on the routing table.Networks
TracerouteA command that shows the intermediate hops a packet passes through to reach the target, useful for diagnosing latency and outages.Networks
ClosureA function that retains the variables of the scope where it was created even after the outer function has finished executing. The basis of callbacks and modules in JavaScript.Programming
Higher-Order FunctionA function that takes a function as input or returns one as output, such as map, filter, and reduce. A foundation of functional programming.Programming
MemoizationCaching the output of an expensive function based on its input and reusing it later. A common optimization for repeated calculations.Programming
ThreadThe smallest unit of execution within a process, sharing memory with other threads. Multithreading speeds up tasks but requires synchronization.Programming
HeapA memory area for dynamically allocating objects at runtime. It differs from the stack, which holds local call frames.Programming
Dependency InjectionPassing dependencies in from outside instead of creating them inside the class. Makes code easier to test and change.Programming
JWTA signed token that carries session data in Base64-encoded chunks. Common in APIs; do not put sensitive secrets inside it.Web
WebhookAn HTTP request sent automatically by a service to your URL when a specific event occurs, instead of you polling constantly.Web
HydrationThe process of attaching JavaScript to server-rendered HTML in the browser to make it interactive. Deferring it improves perceived speed.Web
PWAA website that works like an app: a Service Worker for offline use and a manifest for the icon and home-screen install.Web
NormalizationOrganizing tables to reduce redundancy and inconsistency by splitting them into smaller relations linked by keys.Databases
ShardingDistributing table rows across multiple servers by a specific key. It increases capacity but complicates cross-shard queries.Databases
VLANA logical division of one network into isolated networks on the same switch. It reduces broadcast traffic and limits the spread of an intrusion.Networks
BGPThe routing protocol between Internet providers. It relies on trust, and misconfiguration can isolate an entire network from the Internet.Networks
QoSPrioritizing certain traffic types (calls, video) over types that are less sensitive to delay.Networks
IPv6Uses 128-bit addresses and solves IPv4 address exhaustion. It does not require NAT, but it does require precise firewall rules.Networks
CVSSA 0–10 scale of vulnerability severity (CVE) based on exploitability and impact. Helps prioritize remediation.Security
RCEThe most dangerous class of vulnerability: it lets an attacker run commands on the server and often means complete control of the system.Security
LFI / RFILocal or remote file inclusion vulnerabilities in a web application. They can lead to reading sensitive files or executing code.Security
SSRFA vulnerability that causes the server to execute a request to an address chosen by the attacker, accessing internal services that are not exposed to the Internet.Security
Cyber Kill ChainThe Lockheed Martin attack model: reconnaissance, weaponization, delivery, exploitation, installation, command and control, and actions on objectives. Every stage is an opportunity for detection.Security
Red Team / Blue TeamThe red team attacks during the test and the blue team defends and monitors. The goal is to improve defense, not to win.Security
LinterA tool that reads code before running and detects syntax errors, unused variables, and potential problems.Tools
RegexA syntax for describing, searching, and replacing text. Very powerful but quickly becomes unreadable — build it in steps and test it.Tools
RecursionA function that calls itself to gradually simplify the problem. It needs a base case, otherwise it will crash.Programming
ImmutableA value that cannot be modified after creation. Reduces errors and makes it easier to trace the source of the data.Programming
Type InferenceThe compiler's ability to infer a variable's type from its value without writing the type explicitly.Programming
CallbackA function passed as an argument and executed later when something happens, common in asynchronous events and requests.Programming
Pure FunctionA function that depends only on its input and always returns the same output without side effects. Easier to test.Programming
Garbage CollectorA mechanism that automatically frees unused memory. It exists in Python, Java, and Go, but does not exist in C++ and Rust.Programming
OwnershipRust's memory management system: Every value has one owner, and when it goes out of scope the memory is freed.Programming
GoroutineA lightweight execution path in Go, started with the go keyword and synchronized with others via channels.Programming
TraitA set of methods that define shared behavior in Rust, similar to an interface in other languages.Programming
DependencyAn external library your project depends on. Pinning versions is necessary to reproduce the build.Programming
API EndpointA specific URL that accepts a request and returns data, such as /api/users.Web
JWTA signed token that holds session data. It is used for authentication, and its signature and validity period must be verified.Web
CORSA policy that defines which domains may request resources from another domain in the browser.Web
WebhookA request sent by a server to an address you specify when a specific event occurs, as opposed to an API you call yourself.Web
TransactionA set of operations executed as a single unit: all succeed or all abort (ACID).Databases
IndexA structure that speeds up searching in a specific column in exchange for additional storage space and slower writing.Databases
ORMA layer that turns database tables into objects in code, removing the need to write SQL by hand.Databases
Subnet MaskA number that identifies which part of an IP address represents the network and which part represents the device.Networks
NATNetwork Address Translation: allows local devices to share a single public IP address for Internet access.Networks
LatencyThe time it takes data to move from one point to another. It affects user experience more than download speed.Networks
PayloadThe active part of an attack or message, such as code that exploits a vulnerability or a command executed on the system.Security
Privilege EscalationMoving from a regular user to higher privileges (root) by exploiting a misconfiguration or vulnerability.Security
Zero-dayA vulnerability unknown to the developer and with no fix yet — the most dangerous kind.Security
MakefileA file describing build, test, and run commands, executed with the make command.Tools
No matching terms. Try another word or choose “All.”

🧪 تعلّم بالتطبيق

مختبر كتابة الأكواد

بعد ما تخلص درس اللغة، جرّب الكود هنا مباشرة: عدّل، شغّل، واقرأ الناتج أو الأخطاء. مفيش تثبيت ومفيش إعداد — JavaScript وHTML/CSS بيشتغلوا فورًا، وPython بتتحمّل أول مرة، وC++ بتتجمّع على جهازك.

4 بيئات تجربة
المحررjavascript
الناتجconsole

            
            

              C++ مش هتتنفّذ داخل المتصفح.
              
انسخ الكود واحفظه في ملف main.cpp على كالي، وبعدها جمّعه وشغّله:

              
g++ -std=c++17 main.cpp -o main
./main

              
لو ظهر g++: command not found، ثبّت الأدوات من قسم الأساسيات: sudo apt install build-essential.

            

          

        


        

          
          
          
          جاهز
        

        
اختصار سريع: Ctrl + Enter للتشغيل. الكود بيشتغل في بيئة معزولة، والناتج بيتحدث في نفس الصفحة.

      


      

        

          

          student@guide — terminal
          
        

        

          
الطرفية التعليمية — بيئة آمنة للتعلّم. اكتب help لعرض الأوامر.

          
جرّب: ls · cd projects · cat notes.md · python --version · neofetch

        

        

          
          
        

      

      
الطرفية محاكاة تعليمية داخل الصفحة: نظام ملفات وهمي وأوامر آمنة، ولا تنفّذ أي أمر حقيقي على جهازك.


      








  

    
دليل المبرمجين الشامل — مرجع عربي للتعلّم العملي. للاستخدام التعليمي والقانوني فقط.
